4:T1832,## Summary

Add **BlockRun plugin** enabling ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using [x402 micropayments](https://x402.org) on Base chain.

- **BLOCKRUN_CHAT** action: Pay-per-request access to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and more
- **BLOCKRUN_WALLET** provider: Wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- Automatic x402 payment handling via EIP-712 signatures
- No API keys required - just a wallet with USDC

## How it works

1. Agent makes an AI request via `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action
2. BlockRun gateway returns `402 Payment Required` with price
3. Plugin signs USDC payment (EIP-712 TransferWithAuthorization)
4. Request retries with payment signature
5. AI response returned to agent

Typical cost: **$0.001-0.01 per request**

## Configuration

```env
BASE_CHAIN_WALLET_KEY=0x...  # Private key with USDC on Base
```

## Usage

```typescript
import { blockrunPlugin } from '@elizaos/plugin-blockrun';

const agent = new Agent({
  plugins: [blockrunPlugin],
});
```

## Test plan

- [x] Unit tests for plugin exports
- [x] Wallet provider returns address and balance
- [x] Action validates wallet configuration
- [x] E2E test with real x402 payments (3 successful API calls)

## Links

- [BlockRun](https://blockrun.ai) - Pay-per-request AI gateway
- [x402 Protocol](https://x402.org) - HTTP 402 micropayment standard
- [@blockrun/llm SDK](https://github.com/BlockRunAI/blockrun-llm-ts)

---

cc @w1kke - discussed integration on Twitter

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR adds a BlockRun plugin that enables ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using the x402 micropayment protocol on Base chain. The plugin provides a `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action for making AI API calls and a `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` provider for wallet context.

## What Changed

The PR introduces a complete plugin package (`@elizaos/plugin-blockrun`) with:
- **Action**: `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` - handles AI requests with automatic x402 payment flow
- **Provider**: `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` - supplies wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- **Dependencies**: Uses `@blockrun/llm` SDK for payment handling and `viem` for blockchain interactions
- **Tests**: Comprehensive unit and E2E tests validating the payment flow

## Architecture

The plugin follows ElizaOS patterns with proper action/provider structure:
1. User sends message to agent
2. Action validates wallet configuration
3. LLMClient makes request to BlockRun gateway
4. Gateway returns 402 Payment Required with price
5. Client signs USDC payment via EIP-712
6. Request retries with payment signature
7. AI response returned to agent

## Key Implementation Details

- **Client Caching**: LLMClient instances are cached per agent ID to avoid recreating connections
- **Wallet Configuration**: Supports `BASE_CHAIN_WALLET_KEY` and `BLOCKRUN_WALLET_KEY` settings
- **Error Handling**: Comprehensive try-catch blocks with structured logging
- **Model Support**: OpenAI, Anthropic, Google models via unified interface

## Critical Issues Found

1. **Hardcoded Path** (test-e2e.ts:9): Import uses absolute local path that breaks for other developers
2. **Missing LICENSE**: Package.json references LICENSE file not included in PR
3. **Git Dependency**: Uses git URL instead of published npm package for @blockrun/llm

## Code Quality

The code is well-structured with:
- Proper TypeScript typing throughout
- Good error handling and logging
- Clear documentation and examples
- Comprehensive test coverage including real payment flows
- Follows ElizaOS plugin architecture patterns

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR has good functionality but contains a critical bug that breaks builds for other developers
- Score reflects one critical syntax error (hardcoded local path) that will cause immediate build failures, plus two medium-priority issues (missing LICENSE file and git dependency). The core implementation is solid with proper error handling, validation, and comprehensive tests. Once the hardcoded path is fixed, this would be a 4/5.
- test-e2e.ts requires immediate fix for hardcoded path. package.json needs LICENSE file added and consideration of publishing @blockrun/llm to npm.

<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Agent as ElizaOS Agent
    participant Action as BLOCKRUN_CHAT Action
    participant Client as LLMClient (@blockrun/llm)
    participant Gateway as BlockRun Gateway
    participant Base as Base Chain

    Agent->>Action: User message (prompt)
    Action->>Action: Validate wallet key configured
    Action->>Client: getClient(runtime)
    Note over Client: Cache client by agentId
    
    Action->>Client: chat(model, prompt, options)
    Client->>Gateway: HTTP Request (AI query)
    Gateway-->>Client: 402 Payment Required (price)
    
    Client->>Client: Sign USDC payment (EIP-712)
    Note over Client: TransferWithAuthorization
    
    Client->>Gateway: Retry with payment signature
    Gateway->>Base: Verify signature & transfer USDC
    Base-->>Gateway: Payment confirmed
    
    Gateway->>Gateway: Process AI request
    Gateway-->>Client: AI response
    Client-->>Action: Response text
    
    Action->>Agent: ActionResult with response
    Note over Agent: Display AI response to user
```

<!-- /greptile_comment -->5:T1e68,## Summary

Optimize `runtime.initialize()` performance through atomic upserts and parallelization of independent operations.

**Results:** Cold start -30%, Warm start -40%

## Problem

The current initialization flow has several inefficiencies:
1. Sequential check-then-insert patterns with race condition potential
2. Multiple round-trips to DB for existence checks before inserts
3. Sequential operations that could run in parallel

## Solution

### 1. Atomic Upserts (plugin-sql/base.ts)

Replace check-then-insert with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`:

```typescript
// Before: Two queries + race condition
const existing = await this.getWorld(world.id);
if (existing) throw new Error('World already exists');
await this.db.insert(worldTable).values(world);

// After: Single atomic query
await this.db.insert(worldTable).values(world).onConflictDoNothing();
```

Applied to:
- `createWorld()` - returns silently if world exists
- `createEntities()` - skips existing entities in batch

### 2. Parallel Initialization (core/runtime.ts)

```typescript
// Before: Sequential
await this.ensureWorldExists();
await this.runMigrations();
const entity = await this.getEntityById(entityId);
const room = await this.getRoomById(roomId);

// After: Parallel where possible
await Promise.all([
  this.ensureWorldExists(),
  options?.skipMigrations ? Promise.resolve() : this.runMigrations()
]);

const [entity, room] = await Promise.all([
  this.getEntityById(entityId),
  this.getRoomById(roomId)
]);
```

### 3. Idempotent Ensure Methods

- `ensureWorldExists()` - Always calls `createWorld()`, DB handles duplicates
- `ensureRoomExists()` - Always calls `createRoom()`, DB handles duplicates
- `ensureEntity()` - New method using upsert pattern

### 4. Code Cleanup (server/message.ts)

Removed defensive try/catch blocks that were handling race conditions no longer possible with atomic upserts.

## Files Changed

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` | Added `onConflictDoNothing()` to createWorld, createEntities |
| `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` | Parallel init, extracted `mergeAgentSettings()`, added `ensureEntity()` |
| `packages/server/src/services/message.ts` | Removed unnecessary try/catch blocks |
| `packages/*/src/__tests__/**` | Updated tests for idempotent behavior |

## Benchmark Details

```
Environment: PGLite (in-memory), 20 iterations each

=== COLD START (fresh DB each time) ===
Delta:  -30%

=== WARM START (agent already exists) ===
Delta:  -40%
```

## Test Plan

- [x] `bun test packages/core/src/__tests__/runtime.test.ts` - 47 pass
- [x] `bun test packages/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/unit/` - 147 pass
- [x] `bun test packages/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/` - All suites pass
- [x] Idempotent behavior verified: calling ensure methods multiple times succeeds

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR successfully optimizes runtime initialization by replacing check-then-insert patterns with atomic upserts and parallelizing independent operations, achieving the claimed 30% cold start and 40% warm start improvements.

## Key Changes

- **Atomic Upserts**: Added `.onConflictDoNothing()` to `createWorld()` and `createEntities()` in `plugin-sql/base.ts`, eliminating race conditions and reducing round-trips
- **Parallelization**: Refactored `runtime.initialize()` to execute world creation and migrations in parallel, then entity/room/participant lookups in parallel
- **Code Cleanup**: Removed defensive try/catch blocks in `message.ts` that were handling race conditions no longer possible with atomic operations
- **Refactoring**: Extracted `mergeAgentSettings()` method and added `ensureEntity()` helper for cleaner code organization

## Issues Found

- **`ensureEntity()` implementation** (runtime.ts:2668): Returns the input entity object rather than fetching the actual persisted entity from the database, which differs from typical ensure patterns and could cause issues if callers expect database-populated fields

## Performance Impact

The changes are well-architected for performance:
- Atomic operations reduce DB round-trips from 2+ queries per entity/world to 1
- Parallel execution of independent operations (world creation + migrations, then entity + room + participants) maximizes throughput
- Idempotent operations enable safe retries without application-level locking

Tests properly verify idempotent behavior and preserve-first-write semantics.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with one logic issue requiring clarification
- The atomic upsert pattern and parallelization are well-implemented with proper tests. Score reduced by 1 point due to the `ensureEntity()` implementation question that needs clarification - it may be intentional but differs from expected ensure pattern behavior.
- Pay close attention to `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` - verify the `ensureEntity()` return behavior is intentional

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | Refactored initialization to use `Promise.all()` for parallel execution (world creation and migrations), extracted `mergeAgentSettings()` method, and added `ensureEntity()` helper. One potential issue with `ensureEntity()` always returning the input entity. |
| packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts | Added `onConflictDoNothing()` to `createWorld()` and `createEntities()` for atomic upserts, eliminating race conditions. Clean implementation with proper idempotency. |
| packages/server/src/services/message.ts | Removed defensive try/catch blocks around `ensureWorldExists()` and `ensureRoomExists()` as atomic upserts now handle duplicates at DB level. |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Runtime as AgentRuntime
    participant Adapter as DatabaseAdapter
    participant DB as Database

    Note over Runtime,DB: Cold Start Initialization

    Runtime->>Adapter: ensureAgentExists()
    Adapter->>DB: INSERT agent ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
    DB-->>Adapter: Success
    Adapter-->>Runtime: Agent entity

    Runtime->>Runtime: mergeAgentSettings()
    
    par Parallel Initialization
        Runtime->>Adapter: ensureWorldExists()
        Adapter->>DB: INSERT world ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
        DB-->>Adapter: Success (idempotent)
        Adapter-->>Runtime: World created/exists
    and
        Runtime->>Adapter: runPluginMigrations()
        Adapter->>DB: Run migrations
        DB-->>Adapter: Migrations complete
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Done
    end

    par Parallel Entity/Room Setup
        Runtime->>Adapter: ensureEntity()
        Adapter->>DB: INSERT entity ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING
        DB-->>Adapter: Success (idempotent)
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Entity created/exists
    and
        Runtime->>Adapter: getRoom()
        Adapter->>DB: SELECT room
        DB-->>Adapter: Room data or null
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Room data
    and
        Runtime->>Adapter: getParticipantsForRoom()
        Adapter->>DB: SELECT participants
        DB-->>Adapter: Participant list
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Participants
    end

    alt Room doesn't exist
        Runtime->>Adapter: createRoom()
        Adapter->>DB: INSERT room
        DB-->>Adapter: Success
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Room created
    end

    alt Agent not participant
        Runtime->>Adapter: addParticipant()
        Adapter->>DB: INSERT participant
        DB-->>Adapter: Success
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Participant added
    end

    Note over Runtime,DB: Initialization Complete (-30% cold, -40% warm)
```

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR adds support for configuring the embedding dimension via `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` character setting to skip the ~500ms API call during agent initialization. The implementation simplifies the `ensureEmbeddingDimension` method by checking for a configured dimension first before falling back to the API call.

**Key Changes:**
- Adds `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` setting support in `runtime.ts`
- Refactors `ensureEmbeddingDimension` from 38 to 27 lines
- Adds 4 comprehensive tests covering various scenarios
- Removes duplicate mock setup code in tests

**Critical Issue:**
The implementation is missing validation to ensure the provided dimension is one of the supported values (384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 3072). The database adapter expects these exact values to map to column names (`dim384`, `dim512`, etc.). Invalid dimensions will cause `DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]` to return `undefined`, breaking embedding operations at runtime.

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- Not safe to merge - contains a blocking bug that will break embedding operations with invalid dimension values
- The P0 issue with unsupported dimension values will cause runtime failures when users configure EMBEDDING_DIMENSION to any value outside the supported set. This breaks the core functionality and must be fixed before merging.
- packages/core/src/runtime.ts - needs validation for supported dimension values

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | 2/5 | Added EMBEDDING_DIMENSION config support to skip API call, but missing validation for supported dimension values (384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 3072) |
| packages/core/src/__tests__/runtime.test.ts | 5/5 | Added comprehensive tests for EMBEDDING_DIMENSION feature and cleaned up duplicate mock setup |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant AgentRuntime
    participant Settings
    participant EmbeddingAPI
    participant DatabaseAdapter
    
    User->>AgentRuntime: initialize()
    AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: ensureEmbeddingDimension()
    
    alt EMBEDDING_DIMENSION configured
        AgentRuntime->>Settings: getSetting('EMBEDDING_DIMENSION')
        Settings-->>AgentRuntime: dimension value
        AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: Number(dimension)
        AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: validate parsedDimension > 0
        Note over AgentRuntime: ⚠️ Missing: validate against [384,512,768,1024,1536,3072]
        AgentRuntime->>DatabaseAdapter: ensureEmbeddingDimension(parsedDimension)
        DatabaseAdapter->>DatabaseAdapter: DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]
        Note over DatabaseAdapter: ⚠️ Returns undefined for invalid dimensions
    else No EMBEDDING_DIMENSION configured
        AgentRuntime->>EmbeddingAPI: useModel(TEXT_EMBEDDING, {text: ''})
        EmbeddingAPI-->>AgentRuntime: embedding array
        AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: get embedding.length
        AgentRuntime->>DatabaseAdapter: ensureEmbeddingDimension(length)
        DatabaseAdapter->>DatabaseAdapter: DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]
    end
    
    DatabaseAdapter-->>AgentRuntime: success
    AgentRuntime-->>User: initialized
```

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades recharts from version 2.15.2 to 3.6.0, a major version bump that brings new features, bug fixes, and deprecations.

## Key Changes in recharts 3.6.0

**New Features:**
- New `BarStack` component for configuring stacked bar settings
- Support for ranged stacked bars
- New `equidistantPreserveEnd` interval option for XAxis/YAxis
- Additional TypeScript exports (`TooltipIndex`, `BarRectangleItem`, `DataKey`, etc.)
- `graphicalItemId` added to tooltip payload

**Bug Fixes:**
- Fixed Scatter tooltip payload duplication (not applicable - no Scatter usage)
- Fixed Label/LabelList crash
- Numbers are now rounded before DOM insertion
- Improved shadow DOM support with refs instead of IDs

**Deprecations:**
- `CartesianAxis` is deprecated (not used in this codebase)

## Codebase Impact Assessment

The codebase uses recharts in 3 files:
1. **src/components/ui/chart.tsx** - Wrapper component using `ResponsiveContainer`, `Tooltip`, `Legend`
2. **src/components/daily-activity.tsx** - Stacked bar chart with `Bar`, `BarChart`, `CartesianGrid`, `XAxis`, `YAxis`
3. **src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx** - Line chart with `Line`, `LineChart`, `XAxis`

## Potential Concerns

1. **React 19 Compatibility**: This codebase uses React 19.0.0. Recharts v3.6.0 may not have been explicitly tested with React 19, as React 19 is very recent. This could potentially cause peer dependency warnings or runtime issues.

2. **Major Version Bump**: Moving from 2.x to 3.x typically indicates breaking changes. While the release notes provided don't show obvious breaking changes that affect the current usage patterns, there may be subtle behavioral differences.

3. **XAxis interval prop**: The code uses `interval="preserveStartEnd"` in daily-activity.tsx. The release notes mention a new `equidistantPreserveEnd` option, but it's unclear if `preserveStartEnd` is still valid or if its behavior has changed.

4. **Stacked Bar Radius**: The code uses `radius={[0, 0, 0, 0]}` on individual `Bar` components within a stack. The new `BarStack` component allows configuring radius for the entire stack, which might change how radius is interpreted.

## Recommendations

- The PR checks will run build, lint, and typecheck which should catch most TypeScript incompatibilities
- Consider manual testing of the charts to ensure visual consistency
- Monitor for peer dependency warnings during installation
- If issues arise, the new `BarStack` component could be explored as an alternative to the current stacked bar implementation

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR carries moderate risk due to the major version bump and React 19 compatibility uncertainty
- Score of 3 reflects: (1) Major version upgrade (2.x→3.x) which typically includes breaking changes, (2) Limited but clear documentation of what changed in v3.6.0, (3) Potential React 19 compatibility concerns since React 19 is very recent, (4) Usage is limited to basic chart components (Bar, Line, XAxis, YAxis) which reduces risk surface area, (5) PR checks will validate build and typecheck, (6) No deprecated features are used except possibly uncertainty around interval="preserveStartEnd"
- Pay close attention to src/components/daily-activity.tsx and src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx during testing, as these contain the actual chart implementations that may be affected by behavioral changes

|----------|-------|----------|

<!-- /greptile_comment -->8:T10ae,<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR enhances the profile summary card by adding markdown rendering support using `react-markdown` and `remark-gfm`. The change replaces plain text rendering with proper markdown formatting, allowing headers, lists, links, and other markdown elements in AI-generated contributor summaries to display correctly.

**What Changed:**
- Added `react-markdown` and `remark-gfm` imports
- Replaced `<p>` element with `<div>` wrapped `<ReactMarkdown>` component
- Added Tailwind Typography prose classes for consistent markdown styling
- Configured prose classes to match the card's design system (custom spacing, colors for headings, dark mode support)

**Integration with Codebase:**
The summary content is AI-generated markdown from the pipeline (see `src/lib/pipelines/summarize/aiContributorSummary.ts`), which produces structured markdown with headers, lists, and links. This change ensures that markdown renders correctly in the profile summary cards, matching the pattern already used in `SummaryContent.tsx` for full summary pages.

**Security:**
`react-markdown` is safe by default - it sanitizes HTML and only renders markdown elements unless explicitly configured with `rehype-raw` plugin (which is not used here). The summary data comes from the database, populated by server-side AI generation, so there's no user-controlled input risk.

**Issue Found:**
The existing truncation logic (line 53) slices at character 300, which can break markdown syntax by cutting through links, headers, or other elements. This needs to be addressed with markdown-aware truncation or an alternative approach.

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR is safe to merge with caution - the markdown rendering is implemented correctly, but the truncation logic will cause rendering issues for long lifetime summaries
- Score of 3 reflects that while the core markdown rendering implementation is correct and secure (proper use of react-markdown with GFM support, appropriate styling), there is a logic bug where the existing character-based truncation will break markdown syntax. This issue only affects lifetime summaries longer than 300 characters when not expanded. The bug won't cause crashes or security issues, but will result in malformed markdown rendering. The positive aspects are: libraries are already in dependencies, security is handled correctly by react-markdown's default sanitization, styling matches the design system, and the pattern is consistent with other markdown rendering in the codebase.
- src/components/summary-card.tsx - specifically the truncation logic at lines 48-54 which needs to be made markdown-aware

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>

| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| src/components/summary-card.tsx | 3/5 | Adds markdown rendering to profile summaries but has a critical bug where character-based truncation can break markdown syntax mid-element |

<!-- /greptile_comment -->9:T14af,## Summary

Add BlockRun plugin to the ElizaOS registry.

**Package:** `@blockrun/elizaos-plugin`
**Repository:** https://github.com/BlockRunAI/elizaos-plugin-blockrun
**npm:** https://www.npmjs.com/package/@blockrun/elizaos-plugin

## Description

This plugin enables ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using the [x402 protocol](https://x402.org), paying with USDC micropayments on Base chain. No API keys required - just a wallet with USDC.

**Features:**
- `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action: Pay-per-request access to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and more
- `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` provider: Wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- Automatic x402 payment handling via EIP-712 signatures

**Supported Models:**
- OpenAI: gpt-4o, gpt-4o-mini
- Anthropic: claude-sonnet-4, claude-3.5-haiku
- Google: gemini-2.0-flash

Typical cost: $0.001-0.01 per request

## Links

- [BlockRun](https://blockrun.ai) - Pay-per-request AI gateway
- [x402 Protocol](https://x402.org) - HTTP 402 micropayment standard

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades Zod from version 3.24.1 to 4.3.5, which is a **major version bump** introducing breaking changes.

## Changes Overview
- **Dependency**: `zod` upgraded from `^3.24.1` to `^4.3.5`
- **Type**: Major version upgrade (potentially breaking)

## Critical Issue Found

The codebase uses the `.toLowerCase()` method on a Zod string schema in `src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts:8`, which has been **removed in Zod v4**. This will cause a runtime error when the schema is evaluated.

### Breaking Change Details
In Zod v3, string schemas had convenience methods like `.toLowerCase()` and `.toUpperCase()`. These have been removed in v4 in favor of the more explicit `.transform()` method.

**Current code (will break):**
```typescript
chain: z.string().min(1).toLowerCase()
```

**Required fix:**
```typescript
chain: z.string().min(1).transform(val => val.toLowerCase())
```

## Impact Assessment

### Files Affected
- **Direct impact**: `src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts` (1 location)
- **Indirect impact**: Any code that uses `LinkedWalletSchema` for validation will fail

### Severity
This is a **critical breaking change** that will cause runtime errors in the wallet linking functionality. The application will crash when attempting to validate wallet data.

## Other Compatibility Notes

✅ **Compatible patterns found:**
- `z.object()`, `z.string()`, `z.number()`, `z.array()`, `z.enum()` - all compatible
- `z.nativeEnum()` - still supported in v4
- `.optional()`, `.nullable()`, `.default()` - all compatible
- `.datetime()` - compatible (no options passed)
- `z.infer<>` - compatible

⚠️ **Redundant but working patterns:**
- `.optional().default()` used in 3 locations - works but redundant (`.default()` alone makes field optional)

## Recommendation

**DO NOT MERGE** until the breaking change in `src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts` is fixed. After fixing, thorough testing of the wallet linking feature is required to ensure no runtime errors occur.

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- This PR contains a critical breaking change that will cause runtime errors in production
- Score of 1/5 reflects a major version dependency upgrade with confirmed breaking changes. The codebase uses .toLowerCase() on a Zod schema which has been removed in v4, causing guaranteed runtime failures in wallet linking functionality. The fix is straightforward but mandatory before merge.
- src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts requires immediate attention - line 8 must be updated to use .transform() instead of .toLowerCase()

|----------|-------|----------|

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades `tailwind-merge` from version 2.6.0 to 3.4.0, which brings performance improvements (>10% faster) and support for Tailwind CSS v4.1.5.

## Critical Issue Found

**The `bun.lock` file was not updated** to reflect the new version. The lock file still references `tailwind-merge@2.6.0`, which creates a version mismatch that will cause all CI jobs to fail.

## Analysis

### Current Usage
- `tailwind-merge` is imported once in `src/lib/utils.ts` via the `twMerge` function
- Wrapped in a `cn()` utility function used throughout the codebase in **70+ component files**
- Usage pattern: Standard class merging with `clsx` for conditional classes and `twMerge` for Tailwind class deduplication

### Version Jump (2.6.0 → 3.4.0)
This is a **major version upgrade** from v2 to v3. According to the release notes:
- v3.0.0 introduced breaking changes (not detailed in provided notes)
- v3.1.0 - v3.4.0 added Tailwind CSS v4 support and performance optimizations
- The usage pattern in this codebase (simple `twMerge(clsx(inputs))`) is standard and unlikely affected by breaking changes

### CI/CD Impact
The PR will fail CI checks because:
- `.github/workflows/pr-checks.yml` runs `bun install --frozen-lockfile` (lines 27, 49, 101, 163)
- The `--frozen-lockfile` flag prevents installation if package.json and lock file don't match
- All 4 CI jobs (check, build, test-pipelines, check-migrations) will fail

### Required Action
Run `bun install` locally to update `bun.lock`, then commit both files together.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR cannot be merged as-is due to a critical lock file mismatch that will cause CI failures
- Score of 0 reflects a blocking issue: the bun.lock file was not updated to match the package.json change. This will cause immediate CI failures when `bun install --frozen-lockfile` is executed in all workflow jobs. While the dependency upgrade itself appears safe (tailwind-merge is used in a standard pattern that's unlikely affected by breaking changes), the PR is not deployable without updating the lock file first.
- package.json requires a corresponding bun.lock update - both files must be committed together

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 0/5 | Updated tailwind-merge from 2.6.0 to 3.4.0, but bun.lock file was not updated, causing version mismatch |

</details>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates `@types/node` from 22.10.5 to 25.0.6, a major version jump spanning 3 major versions. While this is a standard Dependabot update, it introduces a significant concern:

**Primary Issue: Runtime/Types Version Mismatch**

The `@types/node` package v25 provides TypeScript type definitions for Node.js 25.x APIs and features. However, this project:
- Uses Next.js 15.4.10, which officially supports Node.js 18.18+ or 20.x (not 25.x)
- Runs CI/CD on ubuntu-latest with Bun, which typically uses Node.js 18 or 20
- Has no explicit Node.js version constraint in package.json

This mismatch means the TypeScript compiler will assume Node.js 25 APIs are available, potentially allowing code that references newer APIs to pass type checking but fail at runtime.

**Impact Assessment**

Current usage of Node.js types in the codebase:
- `NodeJS.ErrnoException` type in error handling (src/lib/fsHelpers.ts)
- `process.env` for environment variables (throughout)  
- Node.js core modules: crypto, fs, path (drizzle/migrate.ts)

These common APIs are stable across versions, so immediate breakage is unlikely. However, future development might inadvertently use Node.js 25-specific features that won't work in the actual runtime.

**Recommendation**

Align `@types/node` version with your actual Node.js runtime version to ensure type safety matches runtime capabilities. If running Node.js 20.x, downgrade to `@types/node@^20.x.x`. If running Node.js 18.x, use `@types/node@^18.x.x`.

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR introduces a runtime/types version mismatch that could cause subtle issues
- Score of 3 reflects moderate risk: the version jump skips 3 major versions and creates a mismatch between type definitions (Node.js 25) and likely runtime (Node.js 18/20). While existing code uses stable APIs that won't break immediately, this mismatch could allow future code to pass type checking but fail at runtime. The PR will likely work but goes against TypeScript best practices of matching types to runtime.
- package.json requires attention to align @types/node version with actual Node.js runtime version

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 3/5 | Major version bump of @types/node from 22.10.5 to 25.0.6 (3 major versions) - potential runtime/types mismatch concern |

</details>

<h3>Remove <code>className</code></h3>
<p>The <code>className</code> prop was removed.
If you want to add classes to some element that wraps the markdown
you can explicitly write that element and add the class to it.
You can then choose yourself which tag name to use and whether to add other
props.</p>
<p>Before:</p>
<pre lang="js"><code>&lt;Markdown className=&quot;markdown-body&quot;&gt;{markdown}&lt;/Markdown&gt;
</code></pre>
<p>After:</p>
<pre lang="js"><code>&lt;div className=&quot;markdown-body&quot;&gt;
  &lt;Markdown&gt;{markdown}&lt;/Markdown&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
</code></pre>
</blockquote>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades `react-markdown` from version 9.1.0 to 10.1.0, which includes several improvements and one breaking change.

## Key Changes in react-markdown 10.x

**Version 10.1.0** (target version):
- Adds `fallback` prop to `MarkdownHooks`
- Fixes race condition in `MarkdownHooks`

**Version 10.0.1**:
- Fixes TypeScript performance around components

**Version 10.0.0** (breaking change):
- **Removed support for `className` prop** - This is a breaking change that requires wrapping the component in a div if you need to apply classes

## Compatibility Analysis

✅ **Good news**: The codebase already follows the recommended pattern:
- The only usage of `ReactMarkdown` is in `src/app/summary/[interval]/[[...date]]/components/SummaryContent.tsx`
- It correctly wraps the component in a `<div>` with the className, rather than passing className directly to `<ReactMarkdown>`
- This means the breaking change will NOT affect this codebase

✅ **Dependencies are compatible**:
- React 19.0.0 is supported (react-markdown 9.1.0 requires >=18)
- remark-gfm@4.0.0 is compatible
- unist-util-visit@5.0.0 is compatible
- Custom remark plugin (`remarkRemoveFirstH1`) uses standard APIs that remain unchanged

## Critical Issue

❌ **The `bun.lock` file was not updated**: This is a critical issue that will prevent the upgrade from taking effect. When `package.json` is modified without updating the lockfile:
- The actual installed version remains at 9.1.0
- CI/CD builds using `--frozen-lockfile` will fail
- This creates version inconsistencies across environments

## Recommendation

The upgrade itself is safe and compatible with the codebase, but the PR is incomplete without the lockfile update. The maintainers should run `bun install` to update `bun.lock` before merging.

### Confidence Score: 2/5

- This PR cannot be safely merged without updating the lockfile - it will cause CI/CD failures
- The dependency upgrade itself is compatible with the codebase (no breaking changes affect the current usage), but the missing lockfile update is a critical blocker. The PR will fail CI checks that use `--frozen-lockfile` flag, and even if merged, the dependency won't actually be upgraded in production builds. Score reflects that the intent is good but the implementation is incomplete.
- package.json requires a corresponding bun.lock update to complete the dependency upgrade

File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 2/5 | Bumps react-markdown from 9.1.0 to 10.1.0, but bun.lock is not updated causing version mismatch |

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR attempts to upgrade `eslint-config-next` from version 15.1.4 to 16.1.1, but this creates a **critical version mismatch** with the current Next.js installation.

## Key Issues Identified

### 1. Major Version Mismatch (Critical)
The project uses **Next.js 15.4.10** while this PR upgrades **eslint-config-next to 16.1.1**. The eslint-config-next package follows Next.js's major versioning scheme, and using v16 ESLint config with Next.js v15 can cause:
- ESLint rules designed for Next.js 16 features that don't exist in v15
- False positive warnings or missing validations
- Inconsistent linting behavior across the codebase

### 2. Missing Lockfile Update (Blocks Merge)
The `bun.lock` file was not updated alongside package.json. This will cause immediate CI failures because all GitHub Actions workflows use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, which will fail when the lockfile doesn't match package.json.

## Recommendation

**Do not merge this PR.** Instead:

1. **Keep eslint-config-next aligned with Next.js version** - Use eslint-config-next 15.x.x to match Next.js 15.4.10
2. **Configure Dependabot** to keep these packages in sync - Consider grouping Next.js and eslint-config-next updates together
3. **When upgrading to Next.js 16** in the future, upgrade both packages together in a single PR

This is a standard Dependabot PR that doesn't account for the version alignment requirement between Next.js and its ESLint configuration package.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR has critical issues and should NOT be merged
- Score of 0 reflects two blocking issues: (1) Major version mismatch between Next.js 15.4.10 and eslint-config-next 16.1.1 that will cause linting inconsistencies and potential false positives, and (2) Missing bun.lock update that will cause immediate CI pipeline failures. Both issues make this PR unsafe to merge.
- package.json requires version alignment - eslint-config-next should match the Next.js major version (15.x), and bun.lock must be updated to reflect dependency changes

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades `p-retry` from version 6.2.1 to 7.1.1, which is a major version bump that includes breaking changes.

**Key Breaking Changes in p-retry v7:**
1. Requires Node.js 20 (this project uses Bun, so runtime compatibility should be verified)
2. `onFailedAttempt` callback now receives a context object where the error must be accessed via `context.error` instead of the callback parameter being the error directly
3. The `forever` option has been removed (not used in this codebase)

**Impact:**
The codebase has two locations in `src/lib/data/github.ts` that use `onFailedAttempt` callbacks and will break with this upgrade:
- Lines 568-582 (GraphQL execution)
- Lines 1092-1106 (REST API execution)

Both callbacks access error properties directly (e.g., `error.message`, `error instanceof RateLimitExceededError`) which will fail in v7 where these need to be accessed via `error.error`.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR will cause runtime failures due to breaking API changes in p-retry v7
- The upgrade from p-retry v6 to v7 introduces breaking changes to the `onFailedAttempt` callback API. The existing code directly accesses error properties that will no longer be available at the top level in v7. This will cause TypeScript compilation errors or runtime failures when the retry logic is triggered. The code needs to be updated to access the error via `context.error` before this dependency upgrade can be safely merged.
- src/lib/data/github.ts requires updates to handle the new p-retry v7 API

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 2/5 | Updates p-retry from 6.2.1 to 7.1.1 - includes breaking API changes that affect existing code |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Dev as Developer
    participant Dep as Dependabot
    participant PJ as package.json
    participant GH as src/lib/data/github.ts
    participant PR as p-retry Library
    
    Dev->>Dep: Automated dependency check
    Dep->>PJ: Update p-retry: 6.2.1 → 7.1.1
    Note over PJ: Major version bump
    
    Note over GH,PR: Breaking Change Impact
    GH->>PR: executeGraphQL() calls pRetry()
    PR-->>GH: onFailedAttempt(context)
    Note over GH: ❌ Accesses error.message<br/>❌ Checks error instanceof<br/>❌ Accesses error.resetAt
    Note over GH: ✅ Should use error.error.message<br/>✅ Should check error.error instanceof<br/>✅ Should access error.error.resetAt
    
    GH->>PR: executeRequest() calls pRetry()
    PR-->>GH: onFailedAttempt(context)
    Note over GH: ❌ Same breaking changes<br/>in REST API handler
    
    Note over Dev: Code must be updated<br/>before merge
```

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates the `lint-staged` development dependency from `^15.5.0` to `^16.2.7`, a major version bump that includes bug fixes and improvements to error handling, task spawning, and the `--continue-on-error` and `--fail-on-changes` options.

**Key Changes:**
- Major version upgrade from v15 to v16 with multiple patch releases (16.2.2 through 16.2.7)
- Bug fixes for task spawning failures, error message handling, and stash management
- Performance improvements (lazy YAML import in v16.2.4)

**Critical Issue:**
The `bun.lock` file was not updated alongside `package.json`. This creates a lockfile mismatch that will cause all CI/CD workflows to fail since they use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`. The PR checks, build, and deployment workflows will all fail until the lockfile is regenerated.

**Configuration Compatibility:**
The existing `.lintstagedrc.mjs` configuration uses ESM syntax with `export default`, which is compatible with lint-staged v16. The configuration format has not changed between v15 and v16, so no config updates are needed.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR cannot be merged as-is - it will cause immediate CI failures across all workflows
- The PR has a critical blocking issue: the bun.lock file was not updated when package.json was changed. All CI/CD workflows (pr-checks.yml, deploy.yml, run-pipelines.yml) use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, which will fail with a lockfile mismatch error. This is not a hypothetical issue - it will definitely break the build. The fix is straightforward (run `bun install` to regenerate the lockfile), but until that's done, this PR blocks all CI processes.
- The bun.lock file must be regenerated and included in this PR. Without it, the PR will break CI/CD pipelines.

|----------|-------|----------|

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates `@types/minimatch` from version 5.1.2 to 6.0.0, which is a **necessary alignment** to match the actual `minimatch` runtime package version (10.0.1) already in use.

## What Changed
- Bumped `@types/minimatch` from `^5.1.2` to `^6.0.0` in dependencies

## Why This Matters
The codebase uses `minimatch` version 10.0.1 (released in 2024), but was using `@types/minimatch` 5.1.2, which provides types for much older versions of minimatch (3.x-5.x). This type definition upgrade brings the TypeScript types in sync with the actual minimatch library being used at runtime.

## Analysis

**Usage Location**: The `minimatch` library and `MinimatchOptions` type are used in `src/lib/matching/matching-logic.ts` for glob pattern matching functionality.

**Options Used**: 
- `nocase`: ✅ Compatible
- `matchBase`: ✅ Compatible  
- `dot`: ✅ Compatible
- `noglobstar`: ✅ Compatible (still supported in minimatch 10.x)
- `nonegate`: ✅ Compatible

**Safety**: This change has been verified to be safe:
1. All MinimatchOptions properties used in the codebase remain valid in minimatch 10.x
2. The PR checks workflow includes TypeScript type checking that would catch any incompatibilities
3. Comprehensive test coverage exists in `src/lib/matching/matching-logic.test.ts`
4. This actually **fixes** a type mismatch where types didn't match the runtime version

### Confidence Score: 5/5

- This PR is safe to merge with no risk - it fixes a type version mismatch
- Score reflects that this is a beneficial type definition update that aligns types with the runtime library. The change has been thoroughly analyzed: all MinimatchOptions properties used remain compatible, CI includes type checking that would catch issues, and comprehensive tests exist. This actually improves type safety rather than introducing risk.
- No files require special attention

|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 5/5 | Type definition bump from @types/minimatch 5.1.2 to 6.0.0 to align with minimatch 10.0.1 runtime; no breaking changes detected |

<!-- /greptile_comment -->12:T425f,Bumps the minor-and-patch group with 1 update: [task-master-ai](https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master).

Updates `task-master-ai` from 0.40.1 to 0.41.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/releases">task-master-ai's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>task-master-ai@0.41.0</h2>
<h3>Minor Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1571">#1571</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/c2d6c18a96fce5a2d5cb50bd1ae5d58ef577501c"><code>c2d6c18</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - Add loop command for automated task execution with Claude Code</p>
<p><strong>New Features:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><code>task-master loop</code> command that runs Claude Code in a Docker sandbox, executing one task per iteration based on the selected tag</li>
<li>Built-in presets for different workflows:
<ul>
<li><code>default</code> - General task completion from the Task Master backlog</li>
<li><code>test-coverage</code> - Find uncovered code and write meaningful tests</li>
<li><code>linting</code> - Fix lint errors and type errors one by one</li>
<li><code>duplication</code> - Find duplicated code and refactor into shared utilities</li>
<li><code>entropy</code> - Find code smells and clean them up</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>Progress file tracking to maintain context across iterations (inside <code>.taskmaster/loop-progress.txt</code>)
<ul>
<li>Remember to delete this file between loops to not pollute the agent with bad context</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>Automatic completion detection via <code>&lt;loop-complete&gt;</code> and <code>&lt;loop-blocked&gt;</code> markers</li>
</ul>
</li>
</ul>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1556">#1556</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/1befc6a341babd825b8dd000513ffbf8a1620e62"><code>1befc6a</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/TheLazyIndianTechie"><code>@​TheLazyIndianTechie</code></a>! - fix: tolerate AI SDK versions without jsonSchema export</p>
<p>Fallback to sanitized Zod schema handling when jsonSchema is unavailable, and
align structured-output tests and registration perf thresholds to reduce CI
failures.</p>
<p>Also enforce sequential, unique subtask ids when regenerating subtasks during
scope adjustment.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1553">#1553</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/226678b93aa01d0e62c0fac852802e9955c7ebd7"><code>226678b</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - fix: Remove .default() from Zod schemas to satisfy OpenAI strict JSON schema validation</p>
<p>This fixes an issue where codex-cli provider (using OpenAI API) would fail with &quot;Missing 'dependencies'&quot; error during task expansion. OpenAI's structured outputs require all properties to be in the 'required' array, but Zod's .default() makes fields optional. The fix removes .default() from schemas and applies defaults at the application level instead.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1543">#1543</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/9a6fa1bd2ab389097f1074fe4a4f779dee8180b6"><code>9a6fa1b</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/triepod-ai"><code>@​triepod-ai</code></a>! - feat: Add tool annotations for improved LLM tool understanding</p>
<p>Added MCP tool annotations (readOnlyHint, destructiveHint, title) to all 12 tools to help LLMs better understand tool behavior and make safer decisions about tool execution.</p>
</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>
</details>

- `@dependabot recreate` will recreate this PR, overwriting any edits that have been made to it
- `@dependabot merge` will merge this PR after your CI passes on it
- `@dependabot squash and merge` will squash and merge this PR after your CI passes on it
- `@dependabot cancel merge` will cancel a previously requested merge and block automerging
- `@dependabot reopen` will reopen this PR if it is closed
- `@dependabot close` will close this PR and stop Dependabot recreating it. You can achieve the same result by closing it manually
- `@dependabot show <dependency name> ignore conditions` will show all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> major version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's major version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's major version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name> minor version` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency's minor version (unless you unignore this specific dependency's minor version or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot ignore <dependency name>` will close this group update PR and stop Dependabot creating any more for the specific dependency (unless you unignore this specific dependency or upgrade to it yourself)
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name>` will remove all of the ignore conditions of the specified dependency
- `@dependabot unignore <dependency name> <ignore condition>` will remove the ignore condition of the specified dependency and ignore conditions

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates the `task-master-ai` devDependency from version 0.40.1 to 0.41.0 in the minor-and-patch group. This is an automated Dependabot PR that bumps the version to include new features and bug fixes from the upstream package.

## What Changed

The update from 0.40.1 to 0.41.0 includes:

**New Features (Minor):**
- New `task-master loop` command for automated task execution with Claude Code in a Docker sandbox
- Built-in presets for different workflows (default, test-coverage, linting, duplication, entropy)
- Progress file tracking at `.taskmaster/loop-progress.txt`
- Automatic completion detection via markers

**Bug Fixes (Patch):**
- Tolerance for AI SDK versions without jsonSchema export
- Removal of `.default()` from Zod schemas for OpenAI strict JSON schema validation compatibility
- Added MCP tool annotations for improved LLM tool understanding
- Sequential, unique subtask IDs during scope adjustment

## Critical Issue Found

**Missing Lock File Update**: The `bun.lock` file was not updated in this PR. While `package.json` specifies `task-master-ai@^0.41.0`, the lock file still pins the dependency to version `0.40.1`. This mismatch means that running `bun install` will install version 0.40.1 instead of the intended 0.41.0, defeating the purpose of this dependency update.

## Compatibility Assessment

Based on the changelog, version 0.41.0 is a **minor version bump** that includes:

**New Features:**
- New `task-master loop` command for automated task execution
- Built-in presets for workflows (test-coverage, linting, duplication, entropy)
- Progress file tracking in `.taskmaster/loop-progress.txt`
- Automatic completion detection markers

**Bug Fixes:**
- Tolerance for AI SDK versions without jsonSchema export
- Removal of `.default()` from Zod schemas for OpenAI compatibility
- Tool annotations for improved LLM understanding

**Impact on this project:**
- This is a **devDependency** used only for development task management
- The project only uses the `tasks:list` command via `bunx task-master list`
- No breaking changes are documented in the release notes
- The new features (loop command, MCP tool annotations, Zod schema fixes) are additive and should not affect existing usage
- The patch fixes improve compatibility with different AI SDK versions and OpenAI API validation

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- This PR cannot be merged safely due to a critical lock file mismatch that will cause incorrect dependency installation
- The confidence score of 1/5 reflects a critical issue that blocks this PR from being merged: the bun.lock file was not updated to reflect the package.json change. This means the dependency version specified in package.json (0.41.0) will not actually be installed - instead, the old version (0.40.1) locked in bun.lock will be used. This defeats the entire purpose of the dependency update and could lead to confusion when developers expect new features from 0.41.0 but get the old version instead. While the dependency update itself appears safe (it's a devDependency with no breaking changes), the incomplete PR makes it unsafe to merge.
- package.json requires bun.lock to be regenerated and included in the PR

1d:T1832,## Summary

Add **BlockRun plugin** enabling ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using [x402 micropayments](https://x402.org) on Base chain.

- **BLOCKRUN_CHAT** action: Pay-per-request access to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and more
- **BLOCKRUN_WALLET** provider: Wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- Automatic x402 payment handling via EIP-712 signatures
- No API keys required - just a wallet with USDC

## How it works

1. Agent makes an AI request via `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action
2. BlockRun gateway returns `402 Payment Required` with price
3. Plugin signs USDC payment (EIP-712 TransferWithAuthorization)
4. Request retries with payment signature
5. AI response returned to agent

Typical cost: **$0.001-0.01 per request**

## Configuration

```env
BASE_CHAIN_WALLET_KEY=0x...  # Private key with USDC on Base
```

## Usage

```typescript
import { blockrunPlugin } from '@elizaos/plugin-blockrun';

const agent = new Agent({
  plugins: [blockrunPlugin],
});
```

## Test plan

- [x] Unit tests for plugin exports
- [x] Wallet provider returns address and balance
- [x] Action validates wallet configuration
- [x] E2E test with real x402 payments (3 successful API calls)

## Links

- [BlockRun](https://blockrun.ai) - Pay-per-request AI gateway
- [x402 Protocol](https://x402.org) - HTTP 402 micropayment standard
- [@blockrun/llm SDK](https://github.com/BlockRunAI/blockrun-llm-ts)

---

cc @w1kke - discussed integration on Twitter

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR adds a BlockRun plugin that enables ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using the x402 micropayment protocol on Base chain. The plugin provides a `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action for making AI API calls and a `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` provider for wallet context.

## What Changed

The PR introduces a complete plugin package (`@elizaos/plugin-blockrun`) with:
- **Action**: `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` - handles AI requests with automatic x402 payment flow
- **Provider**: `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` - supplies wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- **Dependencies**: Uses `@blockrun/llm` SDK for payment handling and `viem` for blockchain interactions
- **Tests**: Comprehensive unit and E2E tests validating the payment flow

## Architecture

The plugin follows ElizaOS patterns with proper action/provider structure:
1. User sends message to agent
2. Action validates wallet configuration
3. LLMClient makes request to BlockRun gateway
4. Gateway returns 402 Payment Required with price
5. Client signs USDC payment via EIP-712
6. Request retries with payment signature
7. AI response returned to agent

## Key Implementation Details

- **Client Caching**: LLMClient instances are cached per agent ID to avoid recreating connections
- **Wallet Configuration**: Supports `BASE_CHAIN_WALLET_KEY` and `BLOCKRUN_WALLET_KEY` settings
- **Error Handling**: Comprehensive try-catch blocks with structured logging
- **Model Support**: OpenAI, Anthropic, Google models via unified interface

## Critical Issues Found

1. **Hardcoded Path** (test-e2e.ts:9): Import uses absolute local path that breaks for other developers
2. **Missing LICENSE**: Package.json references LICENSE file not included in PR
3. **Git Dependency**: Uses git URL instead of published npm package for @blockrun/llm

## Code Quality

The code is well-structured with:
- Proper TypeScript typing throughout
- Good error handling and logging
- Clear documentation and examples
- Comprehensive test coverage including real payment flows
- Follows ElizaOS plugin architecture patterns

<!-- /greptile_comment -->1e:T1e68,## Summary

Optimize `runtime.initialize()` performance through atomic upserts and parallelization of independent operations.

**Results:** Cold start -30%, Warm start -40%

## Problem

The current initialization flow has several inefficiencies:
1. Sequential check-then-insert patterns with race condition potential
2. Multiple round-trips to DB for existence checks before inserts
3. Sequential operations that could run in parallel

## Solution

### 1. Atomic Upserts (plugin-sql/base.ts)

Replace check-then-insert with `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`:

```typescript
// Before: Two queries + race condition
const existing = await this.getWorld(world.id);
if (existing) throw new Error('World already exists');
await this.db.insert(worldTable).values(world);

// After: Single atomic query
await this.db.insert(worldTable).values(world).onConflictDoNothing();
```

Applied to:
- `createWorld()` - returns silently if world exists
- `createEntities()` - skips existing entities in batch

### 2. Parallel Initialization (core/runtime.ts)

```typescript
// Before: Sequential
await this.ensureWorldExists();
await this.runMigrations();
const entity = await this.getEntityById(entityId);
const room = await this.getRoomById(roomId);

// After: Parallel where possible
await Promise.all([
  this.ensureWorldExists(),
  options?.skipMigrations ? Promise.resolve() : this.runMigrations()
]);

const [entity, room] = await Promise.all([
  this.getEntityById(entityId),
  this.getRoomById(roomId)
]);
```

### 3. Idempotent Ensure Methods

- `ensureWorldExists()` - Always calls `createWorld()`, DB handles duplicates
- `ensureRoomExists()` - Always calls `createRoom()`, DB handles duplicates
- `ensureEntity()` - New method using upsert pattern

### 4. Code Cleanup (server/message.ts)

Removed defensive try/catch blocks that were handling race conditions no longer possible with atomic upserts.

## Files Changed

| File | Changes |
|------|---------|
| `packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts` | Added `onConflictDoNothing()` to createWorld, createEntities |
| `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` | Parallel init, extracted `mergeAgentSettings()`, added `ensureEntity()` |
| `packages/server/src/services/message.ts` | Removed unnecessary try/catch blocks |
| `packages/*/src/__tests__/**` | Updated tests for idempotent behavior |

## Benchmark Details

```
Environment: PGLite (in-memory), 20 iterations each

=== COLD START (fresh DB each time) ===
Delta:  -30%

=== WARM START (agent already exists) ===
Delta:  -40%
```

## Test Plan

- [x] `bun test packages/core/src/__tests__/runtime.test.ts` - 47 pass
- [x] `bun test packages/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/unit/` - 147 pass
- [x] `bun test packages/plugin-sql/src/__tests__/integration/` - All suites pass
- [x] Idempotent behavior verified: calling ensure methods multiple times succeeds

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h3>Greptile Summary</h3>


This PR successfully optimizes runtime initialization by replacing check-then-insert patterns with atomic upserts and parallelizing independent operations, achieving the claimed 30% cold start and 40% warm start improvements.

## Key Changes

- **Atomic Upserts**: Added `.onConflictDoNothing()` to `createWorld()` and `createEntities()` in `plugin-sql/base.ts`, eliminating race conditions and reducing round-trips
- **Parallelization**: Refactored `runtime.initialize()` to execute world creation and migrations in parallel, then entity/room/participant lookups in parallel
- **Code Cleanup**: Removed defensive try/catch blocks in `message.ts` that were handling race conditions no longer possible with atomic operations
- **Refactoring**: Extracted `mergeAgentSettings()` method and added `ensureEntity()` helper for cleaner code organization

## Issues Found

- **`ensureEntity()` implementation** (runtime.ts:2668): Returns the input entity object rather than fetching the actual persisted entity from the database, which differs from typical ensure patterns and could cause issues if callers expect database-populated fields

## Performance Impact

The changes are well-architected for performance:
- Atomic operations reduce DB round-trips from 2+ queries per entity/world to 1
- Parallel execution of independent operations (world creation + migrations, then entity + room + participants) maximizes throughput
- Idempotent operations enable safe retries without application-level locking

Tests properly verify idempotent behavior and preserve-first-write semantics.

<h3>Confidence Score: 4/5</h3>


- This PR is safe to merge with one logic issue requiring clarification
- The atomic upsert pattern and parallelization are well-implemented with proper tests. Score reduced by 1 point due to the `ensureEntity()` implementation question that needs clarification - it may be intentional but differs from expected ensure pattern behavior.
- Pay close attention to `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` - verify the `ensureEntity()` return behavior is intentional

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>




| Filename | Overview |
|----------|----------|
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | Refactored initialization to use `Promise.all()` for parallel execution (world creation and migrations), extracted `mergeAgentSettings()` method, and added `ensureEntity()` helper. One potential issue with `ensureEntity()` always returning the input entity. |
| packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts | Added `onConflictDoNothing()` to `createWorld()` and `createEntities()` for atomic upserts, eliminating race conditions. Clean implementation with proper idempotency. |
| packages/server/src/services/message.ts | Removed defensive try/catch blocks around `ensureWorldExists()` and `ensureRoomExists()` as atomic upserts now handle duplicates at DB level. |

</details>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Runtime as AgentRuntime
    participant Adapter as DatabaseAdapter

    Note over Runtime,DB: Cold Start Initialization

    Adapter->>DB: INSERT agent ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING

    Runtime->>Runtime: mergeAgentSettings()
    
    par Parallel Initialization

    and
        Runtime->>Adapter: runPluginMigrations()

        Adapter-->>Runtime: Done
    end

        DB-->>Adapter: Success (idempotent)
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Entity created/exists
    and
        Runtime->>Adapter: getRoom()
        Adapter->>DB: SELECT room
        DB-->>Adapter: Room data or null
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Room data
    and
        Runtime->>Adapter: getParticipantsForRoom()
        Adapter->>DB: SELECT participants
        DB-->>Adapter: Participant list
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Participants
    end

    alt Room doesn't exist
        Runtime->>Adapter: createRoom()
        Adapter->>DB: INSERT room
        DB-->>Adapter: Success
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Room created
    end

    alt Agent not participant
        Runtime->>Adapter: addParticipant()
        Adapter->>DB: INSERT participant
        DB-->>Adapter: Success
        Adapter-->>Runtime: Participant added
    end

    Note over Runtime,DB: Initialization Complete (-30% cold, -40% warm)
```

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR adds support for configuring the embedding dimension via `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` character setting to skip the ~500ms API call during agent initialization. The implementation simplifies the `ensureEmbeddingDimension` method by checking for a configured dimension first before falling back to the API call.

**Key Changes:**
- Adds `EMBEDDING_DIMENSION` setting support in `runtime.ts`
- Refactors `ensureEmbeddingDimension` from 38 to 27 lines
- Adds 4 comprehensive tests covering various scenarios
- Removes duplicate mock setup code in tests

**Critical Issue:**
The implementation is missing validation to ensure the provided dimension is one of the supported values (384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 3072). The database adapter expects these exact values to map to column names (`dim384`, `dim512`, etc.). Invalid dimensions will cause `DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]` to return `undefined`, breaking embedding operations at runtime.

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- Not safe to merge - contains a blocking bug that will break embedding operations with invalid dimension values
- The P0 issue with unsupported dimension values will cause runtime failures when users configure EMBEDDING_DIMENSION to any value outside the supported set. This breaks the core functionality and must be fixed before merging.
- packages/core/src/runtime.ts - needs validation for supported dimension values

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| packages/core/src/runtime.ts | 2/5 | Added EMBEDDING_DIMENSION config support to skip API call, but missing validation for supported dimension values (384, 512, 768, 1024, 1536, 3072) |
| packages/core/src/__tests__/runtime.test.ts | 5/5 | Added comprehensive tests for EMBEDDING_DIMENSION feature and cleaned up duplicate mock setup |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant User
    participant AgentRuntime
    participant Settings
    participant EmbeddingAPI
    participant DatabaseAdapter
    
    User->>AgentRuntime: initialize()
    AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: ensureEmbeddingDimension()
    
    alt EMBEDDING_DIMENSION configured
        AgentRuntime->>Settings: getSetting('EMBEDDING_DIMENSION')
        Settings-->>AgentRuntime: dimension value
        AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: Number(dimension)
        AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: validate parsedDimension > 0
        Note over AgentRuntime: ⚠️ Missing: validate against [384,512,768,1024,1536,3072]
        AgentRuntime->>DatabaseAdapter: ensureEmbeddingDimension(parsedDimension)
        DatabaseAdapter->>DatabaseAdapter: DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]
        Note over DatabaseAdapter: ⚠️ Returns undefined for invalid dimensions
    else No EMBEDDING_DIMENSION configured
        AgentRuntime->>EmbeddingAPI: useModel(TEXT_EMBEDDING, {text: ''})
        EmbeddingAPI-->>AgentRuntime: embedding array
        AgentRuntime->>AgentRuntime: get embedding.length
        AgentRuntime->>DatabaseAdapter: ensureEmbeddingDimension(length)
        DatabaseAdapter->>DatabaseAdapter: DIMENSION_MAP[dimension]
    end
    
    DatabaseAdapter-->>AgentRuntime: success
    AgentRuntime-->>User: initialized
```

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades recharts from version 2.15.2 to 3.6.0, a major version bump that brings new features, bug fixes, and deprecations.

## Key Changes in recharts 3.6.0

**New Features:**
- New `BarStack` component for configuring stacked bar settings
- Support for ranged stacked bars
- New `equidistantPreserveEnd` interval option for XAxis/YAxis
- Additional TypeScript exports (`TooltipIndex`, `BarRectangleItem`, `DataKey`, etc.)
- `graphicalItemId` added to tooltip payload

**Bug Fixes:**
- Fixed Scatter tooltip payload duplication (not applicable - no Scatter usage)
- Fixed Label/LabelList crash
- Numbers are now rounded before DOM insertion
- Improved shadow DOM support with refs instead of IDs

**Deprecations:**
- `CartesianAxis` is deprecated (not used in this codebase)

## Codebase Impact Assessment

The codebase uses recharts in 3 files:
1. **src/components/ui/chart.tsx** - Wrapper component using `ResponsiveContainer`, `Tooltip`, `Legend`
2. **src/components/daily-activity.tsx** - Stacked bar chart with `Bar`, `BarChart`, `CartesianGrid`, `XAxis`, `YAxis`
3. **src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx** - Line chart with `Line`, `LineChart`, `XAxis`

## Potential Concerns

1. **React 19 Compatibility**: This codebase uses React 19.0.0. Recharts v3.6.0 may not have been explicitly tested with React 19, as React 19 is very recent. This could potentially cause peer dependency warnings or runtime issues.

2. **Major Version Bump**: Moving from 2.x to 3.x typically indicates breaking changes. While the release notes provided don't show obvious breaking changes that affect the current usage patterns, there may be subtle behavioral differences.

3. **XAxis interval prop**: The code uses `interval="preserveStartEnd"` in daily-activity.tsx. The release notes mention a new `equidistantPreserveEnd` option, but it's unclear if `preserveStartEnd` is still valid or if its behavior has changed.

4. **Stacked Bar Radius**: The code uses `radius={[0, 0, 0, 0]}` on individual `Bar` components within a stack. The new `BarStack` component allows configuring radius for the entire stack, which might change how radius is interpreted.

## Recommendations

- The PR checks will run build, lint, and typecheck which should catch most TypeScript incompatibilities
- Consider manual testing of the charts to ensure visual consistency
- Monitor for peer dependency warnings during installation
- If issues arise, the new `BarStack` component could be explored as an alternative to the current stacked bar implementation

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR carries moderate risk due to the major version bump and React 19 compatibility uncertainty
- Score of 3 reflects: (1) Major version upgrade (2.x→3.x) which typically includes breaking changes, (2) Limited but clear documentation of what changed in v3.6.0, (3) Potential React 19 compatibility concerns since React 19 is very recent, (4) Usage is limited to basic chart components (Bar, Line, XAxis, YAxis) which reduces risk surface area, (5) PR checks will validate build and typecheck, (6) No deprecated features are used except possibly uncertainty around interval="preserveStartEnd"
- Pay close attention to src/components/daily-activity.tsx and src/app/repos/components/RepositoryCard.tsx during testing, as these contain the actual chart implementations that may be affected by behavioral changes

The change replaces plain text rendering with proper markdown formatting, allowing headers, lists, links, and other markdown elements in AI-generated contributor summaries to display correctly.

- Replaced `<p>` element with `<div>` wrapped `<ReactMarkdown>` component
- Added Tailwind Typography prose classes for consistent markdown styling
- Configured prose classes to match the card's design system (custom spacing, colors for headings, dark mode support)

The summary content is AI-generated markdown from the pipeline (see `src/lib/pipelines/summarize/aiContributorSummary.ts`), which produces structured markdown with headers, lists, and links. This change ensures that markdown renders correctly in the profile summary cards, matching the pattern already used in `SummaryContent.tsx` for full summary pages.

**Security:**
`react-markdown` is safe by default - it sanitizes HTML and only renders markdown elements unless explicitly configured with `rehype-raw` plugin (which is not used here). The summary data comes from the database, populated by server-side AI generation, so there's no user-controlled input risk.

**Issue Found:**
The existing truncation logic (line 53) slices at character 300, which can break markdown syntax by cutting through links, headers, or other elements. This needs to be addressed with markdown-aware truncation or an alternative approach.

The positive aspects are: libraries are already in dependencies, security is handled correctly by react-markdown's default sanitization, styling matches the design system, and the pattern is consistent with other markdown rendering in the codebase.

<!-- /greptile_comment -->22:T14af,## Summary

Add BlockRun plugin to the ElizaOS registry.

**Package:** `@blockrun/elizaos-plugin`
**Repository:** https://github.com/BlockRunAI/elizaos-plugin-blockrun
**npm:** https://www.npmjs.com/package/@blockrun/elizaos-plugin

## Description

This plugin enables ElizaOS agents to make pay-per-request AI calls using the [x402 protocol](https://x402.org), paying with USDC micropayments on Base chain. No API keys required - just a wallet with USDC.

**Features:**
- `BLOCKRUN_CHAT` action: Pay-per-request access to OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, and more
- `BLOCKRUN_WALLET` provider: Wallet address and USDC balance on Base
- Automatic x402 payment handling via EIP-712 signatures

**Supported Models:**
- OpenAI: gpt-4o, gpt-4o-mini
- Anthropic: claude-sonnet-4, claude-3.5-haiku
- Google: gemini-2.0-flash

Typical cost: $0.001-0.01 per request

## Links

- [BlockRun](https://blockrun.ai) - Pay-per-request AI gateway
- [x402 Protocol](https://x402.org) - HTTP 402 micropayment standard

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades Zod from version 3.24.1 to 4.3.5, which is a **major version bump** introducing breaking changes.

## Changes Overview
- **Dependency**: `zod` upgraded from `^3.24.1` to `^4.3.5`
- **Type**: Major version upgrade (potentially breaking)

## Critical Issue Found

The codebase uses the `.toLowerCase()` method on a Zod string schema in `src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts:8`, which has been **removed in Zod v4**. This will cause a runtime error when the schema is evaluated.

### Breaking Change Details
In Zod v3, string schemas had convenience methods like `.toLowerCase()` and `.toUpperCase()`. These have been removed in v4 in favor of the more explicit `.transform()` method.

**Current code (will break):**
```typescript
chain: z.string().min(1).toLowerCase()
```

**Required fix:**
```typescript
chain: z.string().min(1).transform(val => val.toLowerCase())
```

## Impact Assessment

### Files Affected
- **Direct impact**: `src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts` (1 location)
- **Indirect impact**: Any code that uses `LinkedWalletSchema` for validation will fail

### Severity
This is a **critical breaking change** that will cause runtime errors in the wallet linking functionality. The application will crash when attempting to validate wallet data.

## Other Compatibility Notes

✅ **Compatible patterns found:**
- `z.object()`, `z.string()`, `z.number()`, `z.array()`, `z.enum()` - all compatible
- `z.nativeEnum()` - still supported in v4
- `.optional()`, `.nullable()`, `.default()` - all compatible
- `.datetime()` - compatible (no options passed)
- `z.infer<>` - compatible

⚠️ **Redundant but working patterns:**
- `.optional().default()` used in 3 locations - works but redundant (`.default()` alone makes field optional)

## Recommendation

**DO NOT MERGE** until the breaking change in `src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts` is fixed. After fixing, thorough testing of the wallet linking feature is required to ensure no runtime errors occur.

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- This PR contains a critical breaking change that will cause runtime errors in production
- Score of 1/5 reflects a major version dependency upgrade with confirmed breaking changes. The codebase uses .toLowerCase() on a Zod schema which has been removed in v4, causing guaranteed runtime failures in wallet linking functionality. The fix is straightforward but mandatory before merge.
- src/lib/walletLinking/readmeUtils.ts requires immediate attention - line 8 must be updated to use .transform() instead of .toLowerCase()

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 2/5 | Zod upgrade from 3.24.1 to 4.3.5 introduces breaking changes; .toLowerCase() method used in codebase is removed in v4 |

</details>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades `tailwind-merge` from version 2.6.0 to 3.4.0, which brings performance improvements (>10% faster) and support for Tailwind CSS v4.1.5.

## Critical Issue Found

**The `bun.lock` file was not updated** to reflect the new version. The lock file still references `tailwind-merge@2.6.0`, which creates a version mismatch that will cause all CI jobs to fail.

## Analysis

### Current Usage
- `tailwind-merge` is imported once in `src/lib/utils.ts` via the `twMerge` function
- Wrapped in a `cn()` utility function used throughout the codebase in **70+ component files**
- Usage pattern: Standard class merging with `clsx` for conditional classes and `twMerge` for Tailwind class deduplication

### Version Jump (2.6.0 → 3.4.0)
This is a **major version upgrade** from v2 to v3. According to the release notes:
- v3.0.0 introduced breaking changes (not detailed in provided notes)
- v3.1.0 - v3.4.0 added Tailwind CSS v4 support and performance optimizations
- The usage pattern in this codebase (simple `twMerge(clsx(inputs))`) is standard and unlikely affected by breaking changes

### CI/CD Impact
The PR will fail CI checks because:
- `.github/workflows/pr-checks.yml` runs `bun install --frozen-lockfile` (lines 27, 49, 101, 163)
- The `--frozen-lockfile` flag prevents installation if package.json and lock file don't match
- All 4 CI jobs (check, build, test-pipelines, check-migrations) will fail

### Required Action
Run `bun install` locally to update `bun.lock`, then commit both files together.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR cannot be merged as-is due to a critical lock file mismatch that will cause CI failures
- Score of 0 reflects a blocking issue: the bun.lock file was not updated to match the package.json change. This will cause immediate CI failures when `bun install --frozen-lockfile` is executed in all workflow jobs. While the dependency upgrade itself appears safe (tailwind-merge is used in a standard pattern that's unlikely affected by breaking changes), the PR is not deployable without updating the lock file first.
- package.json requires a corresponding bun.lock update - both files must be committed together

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 0/5 | Updated tailwind-merge from 2.6.0 to 3.4.0, but bun.lock file was not updated, causing version mismatch |

</details>

```mermaid
    CI->>LOCK: Run `bun install --frozen-lockfile`
    LOCK-->>CI: ✓ Success
    CI->>Runtime: Deploy with tailwind-merge@3.4.0

    Note over Runtime: Used in 70+ component files
```
