<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates `@types/node` from 22.10.5 to 25.0.6, a major version jump spanning 3 major versions. While this is a standard Dependabot update, it introduces a significant concern:

**Primary Issue: Runtime/Types Version Mismatch**

The `@types/node` package v25 provides TypeScript type definitions for Node.js 25.x APIs and features. However, this project:
- Uses Next.js 15.4.10, which officially supports Node.js 18.18+ or 20.x (not 25.x)
- Runs CI/CD on ubuntu-latest with Bun, which typically uses Node.js 18 or 20
- Has no explicit Node.js version constraint in package.json

This mismatch means the TypeScript compiler will assume Node.js 25 APIs are available, potentially allowing code that references newer APIs to pass type checking but fail at runtime.

**Impact Assessment**

Current usage of Node.js types in the codebase:
- `NodeJS.ErrnoException` type in error handling (src/lib/fsHelpers.ts)
- `process.env` for environment variables (throughout)  
- Node.js core modules: crypto, fs, path (drizzle/migrate.ts)

These common APIs are stable across versions, so immediate breakage is unlikely. However, future development might inadvertently use Node.js 25-specific features that won't work in the actual runtime.

**Recommendation**

Align `@types/node` version with your actual Node.js runtime version to ensure type safety matches runtime capabilities. If running Node.js 20.x, downgrade to `@types/node@^20.x.x`. If running Node.js 18.x, use `@types/node@^18.x.x`.

### Confidence Score: 3/5

- This PR introduces a runtime/types version mismatch that could cause subtle issues
- Score of 3 reflects moderate risk: the version jump skips 3 major versions and creates a mismatch between type definitions (Node.js 25) and likely runtime (Node.js 18/20). While existing code uses stable APIs that won't break immediately, this mismatch could allow future code to pass type checking but fail at runtime. The PR will likely work but goes against TypeScript best practices of matching types to runtime.
- package.json requires attention to align @types/node version with actual Node.js runtime version

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 3/5 | Major version bump of @types/node from 22.10.5 to 25.0.6 (3 major versions) - potential runtime/types mismatch concern |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant Dev as Developer
    participant Dep as Dependabot
    participant CI as CI/CD (Node 18/20)
    participant TS as TypeScript Compiler
    participant RT as Runtime (Bun/Node)
    
    Dep->>Dep: Detect @types/node update available
    Dep->>Dev: Create PR: v22.10.5 → v25.0.6
    Note over Dep,Dev: 3 major version jump
    
    Dev->>CI: Merge PR
    CI->>CI: Install dependencies (bun install)
    CI->>TS: Run typecheck
    Note over TS: Uses @types/node v25 definitions<br/>(Node.js 25.x types)
    TS->>TS: Type check passes
    Note over TS: Assumes Node.js 25 APIs available
    
    CI->>CI: Run build (Next.js)
    CI->>RT: Execute on Node.js 18/20
    Note over RT: Runtime has older APIs<br/>than types suggest
    
    alt If using Node 25 specific APIs
        RT-->>CI: Potential runtime error
        Note over RT: API not available in Node 18/20
    else Using stable APIs (current case)
        RT->>CI: Success
        Note over RT: Common APIs work across versions
    end
```

<details>
<summary>Changelog</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/remarkjs/react-markdown/blob/main/changelog.md">react-markdown's changelog</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<!-- raw HTML omitted -->
<h1>Changelog</h1>
<p>All notable changes will be documented in this file.</p>
<h2>10.0.0 - 2025-02-20</h2>
<ul>
<li><a href="https://github.com/remarkjs/react-markdown/commit/aaaa40b"><code>aaaa40b</code></a>
Remove support for <code>className</code> prop
<strong>migrate</strong>: see “Remove <code>className</code>” below</li>
</ul>
<h3>Remove <code>className</code></h3>
<p>The <code>className</code> prop was removed.
If you want to add classes to some element that wraps the markdown
you can explicitly write that element and add the class to it.
You can then choose yourself which tag name to use and whether to add other
props.</p>
<p>Before:</p>
<pre lang="js"><code>&lt;Markdown className=&quot;markdown-body&quot;&gt;{markdown}&lt;/Markdown&gt;
</code></pre>
<p>After:</p>
<pre lang="js"><code>&lt;div className=&quot;markdown-body&quot;&gt;
  &lt;Markdown&gt;{markdown}&lt;/Markdown&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
</code></pre>
</blockquote>
</details>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades `react-markdown` from version 9.1.0 to 10.1.0, which includes several improvements and one breaking change.

## Key Changes in react-markdown 10.x

**Version 10.1.0** (target version):
- Adds `fallback` prop to `MarkdownHooks`
- Fixes race condition in `MarkdownHooks`

**Version 10.0.1**:
- Fixes TypeScript performance around components

**Version 10.0.0** (breaking change):
- **Removed support for `className` prop** - This is a breaking change that requires wrapping the component in a div if you need to apply classes

## Compatibility Analysis

✅ **Good news**: The codebase already follows the recommended pattern:
- The only usage of `ReactMarkdown` is in `src/app/summary/[interval]/[[...date]]/components/SummaryContent.tsx`
- It correctly wraps the component in a `<div>` with the className, rather than passing className directly to `<ReactMarkdown>`
- This means the breaking change will NOT affect this codebase

✅ **Dependencies are compatible**:
- React 19.0.0 is supported (react-markdown 9.1.0 requires >=18)
- remark-gfm@4.0.0 is compatible
- unist-util-visit@5.0.0 is compatible
- Custom remark plugin (`remarkRemoveFirstH1`) uses standard APIs that remain unchanged

## Critical Issue

❌ **The `bun.lock` file was not updated**: This is a critical issue that will prevent the upgrade from taking effect. When `package.json` is modified without updating the lockfile:
- The actual installed version remains at 9.1.0
- CI/CD builds using `--frozen-lockfile` will fail
- This creates version inconsistencies across environments

## Recommendation

The upgrade itself is safe and compatible with the codebase, but the PR is incomplete without the lockfile update. The maintainers should run `bun install` to update `bun.lock` before merging.

### Confidence Score: 2/5

- This PR cannot be safely merged without updating the lockfile - it will cause CI/CD failures
- The dependency upgrade itself is compatible with the codebase (no breaking changes affect the current usage), but the missing lockfile update is a critical blocker. The PR will fail CI checks that use `--frozen-lockfile` flag, and even if merged, the dependency won't actually be upgraded in production builds. Score reflects that the intent is good but the implementation is incomplete.
- package.json requires a corresponding bun.lock update to complete the dependency upgrade

|----------|-------|----------|

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR attempts to upgrade `eslint-config-next` from version 15.1.4 to 16.1.1, but this creates a **critical version mismatch** with the current Next.js installation.

## Key Issues Identified

### 1. Major Version Mismatch (Critical)
The project uses **Next.js 15.4.10** while this PR upgrades **eslint-config-next to 16.1.1**. The eslint-config-next package follows Next.js's major versioning scheme, and using v16 ESLint config with Next.js v15 can cause:
- ESLint rules designed for Next.js 16 features that don't exist in v15
- False positive warnings or missing validations
- Inconsistent linting behavior across the codebase

### 2. Missing Lockfile Update (Blocks Merge)
The `bun.lock` file was not updated alongside package.json. This will cause immediate CI failures because all GitHub Actions workflows use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, which will fail when the lockfile doesn't match package.json.

## Recommendation

**Do not merge this PR.** Instead:

1. **Keep eslint-config-next aligned with Next.js version** - Use eslint-config-next 15.x.x to match Next.js 15.4.10
2. **Configure Dependabot** to keep these packages in sync - Consider grouping Next.js and eslint-config-next updates together
3. **When upgrading to Next.js 16** in the future, upgrade both packages together in a single PR

This is a standard Dependabot PR that doesn't account for the version alignment requirement between Next.js and its ESLint configuration package.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR has critical issues and should NOT be merged
- Score of 0 reflects two blocking issues: (1) Major version mismatch between Next.js 15.4.10 and eslint-config-next 16.1.1 that will cause linting inconsistencies and potential false positives, and (2) Missing bun.lock update that will cause immediate CI pipeline failures. Both issues make this PR unsafe to merge.
- package.json requires version alignment - eslint-config-next should match the Next.js major version (15.x), and bun.lock must be updated to reflect dependency changes

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 2/5 | Major version mismatch: eslint-config-next 16.1.1 upgraded while Next.js remains at 15.4.10, creating potential compatibility issues |

</details>

```mermaid

    participant Dev as Developer
    participant Dep as Dependabot
    participant CI as GitHub Actions
    participant Pkg as package.json
    participant Lock as bun.lock
    participant Next as Next.js v15.4.10
    participant ESLint as eslint-config-next

    Dev->>Next: Uses Next.js 15.4.10
    Dep->>Pkg: Updates eslint-config-next 15.1.4 → 16.1.1
    Note over Dep,Lock: ⚠️ bun.lock NOT updated
    
    CI->>Lock: bun install --frozen-lockfile
    Lock-->>CI: ❌ Lockfile mismatch error
    Note over CI: PR checks will fail

        ESLint-->>CI: Potential lint errors
    end

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR upgrades `p-retry` from version 6.2.1 to 7.1.1, which is a major version bump that includes breaking changes.

**Key Breaking Changes in p-retry v7:**
1. Requires Node.js 20 (this project uses Bun, so runtime compatibility should be verified)
2. `onFailedAttempt` callback now receives a context object where the error must be accessed via `context.error` instead of the callback parameter being the error directly
3. The `forever` option has been removed (not used in this codebase)

**Impact:**
The codebase has two locations in `src/lib/data/github.ts` that use `onFailedAttempt` callbacks and will break with this upgrade:
- Lines 568-582 (GraphQL execution)
- Lines 1092-1106 (REST API execution)

Both callbacks access error properties directly (e.g., `error.message`, `error instanceof RateLimitExceededError`) which will fail in v7 where these need to be accessed via `error.error`.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR will cause runtime failures due to breaking API changes in p-retry v7
- The upgrade from p-retry v6 to v7 introduces breaking changes to the `onFailedAttempt` callback API. The existing code directly accesses error properties that will no longer be available at the top level in v7. This will cause TypeScript compilation errors or runtime failures when the retry logic is triggered. The code needs to be updated to access the error via `context.error` before this dependency upgrade can be safely merged.
- src/lib/data/github.ts requires updates to handle the new p-retry v7 API

c18d55a3 On main: lint-staged automatic backup

<!-- greptile_comment -->

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates the `lint-staged` development dependency from `^15.5.0` to `^16.2.7`, a major version bump that includes bug fixes and improvements to error handling, task spawning, and the `--continue-on-error` and `--fail-on-changes` options.

**Key Changes:**
- Major version upgrade from v15 to v16 with multiple patch releases (16.2.2 through 16.2.7)
- Bug fixes for task spawning failures, error message handling, and stash management
- Performance improvements (lazy YAML import in v16.2.4)

**Critical Issue:**
The `bun.lock` file was not updated alongside `package.json`. This creates a lockfile mismatch that will cause all CI/CD workflows to fail since they use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`. The PR checks, build, and deployment workflows will all fail until the lockfile is regenerated.

**Configuration Compatibility:**
The existing `.lintstagedrc.mjs` configuration uses ESM syntax with `export default`, which is compatible with lint-staged v16. The configuration format has not changed between v15 and v16, so no config updates are needed.

### Confidence Score: 0/5

- This PR cannot be merged as-is - it will cause immediate CI failures across all workflows
- The PR has a critical blocking issue: the bun.lock file was not updated when package.json was changed. All CI/CD workflows (pr-checks.yml, deploy.yml, run-pipelines.yml) use `bun install --frozen-lockfile`, which will fail with a lockfile mismatch error. This is not a hypothetical issue - it will definitely break the build. The fix is straightforward (run `bun install` to regenerate the lockfile), but until that's done, this PR blocks all CI processes.
- The bun.lock file must be regenerated and included in this PR. Without it, the PR will break CI/CD pipelines.

<h3>Important Files Changed</h3>



File Analysis



| Filename | Score | Overview |
|----------|-------|----------|
| package.json | 1/5 | Updates lint-staged from ^15.5.0 to ^16.2.7, but bun.lock was not regenerated - will cause CI failures |

</details>



<h3>Sequence Diagram</h3>

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
    participant D as Dependabot
    participant PR as Pull Request
    participant CI as CI/CD (GitHub Actions)
    participant B as Bun Package Manager
    participant LS as lint-staged
    participant H as Husky Pre-commit Hook

    Note over D,PR: Dependency Update Process

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates `@types/minimatch` from version 5.1.2 to 6.0.0, which is a **necessary alignment** to match the actual `minimatch` runtime package version (10.0.1) already in use.

## What Changed
- Bumped `@types/minimatch` from `^5.1.2` to `^6.0.0` in dependencies

## Why This Matters
The codebase uses `minimatch` version 10.0.1 (released in 2024), but was using `@types/minimatch` 5.1.2, which provides types for much older versions of minimatch (3.x-5.x). This type definition upgrade brings the TypeScript types in sync with the actual minimatch library being used at runtime.

## Analysis

**Usage Location**: The `minimatch` library and `MinimatchOptions` type are used in `src/lib/matching/matching-logic.ts` for glob pattern matching functionality.

**Options Used**: 
- `nocase`: ✅ Compatible
- `matchBase`: ✅ Compatible  
- `dot`: ✅ Compatible
- `noglobstar`: ✅ Compatible (still supported in minimatch 10.x)
- `nonegate`: ✅ Compatible

**Safety**: This change has been verified to be safe:
1. All MinimatchOptions properties used in the codebase remain valid in minimatch 10.x
2. The PR checks workflow includes TypeScript type checking that would catch any incompatibilities
3. Comprehensive test coverage exists in `src/lib/matching/matching-logic.test.ts`
4. This actually **fixes** a type mismatch where types didn't match the runtime version

### Confidence Score: 5/5

- This PR is safe to merge with no risk - it fixes a type version mismatch
- Score reflects that this is a beneficial type definition update that aligns types with the runtime library. The change has been thoroughly analyzed: all MinimatchOptions properties used remain compatible, CI includes type checking that would catch issues, and comprehensive tests exist. This actually improves type safety rather than introducing risk.
- No files require special attention

Updates `task-master-ai` from 0.40.1 to 0.41.0
<details>
<summary>Release notes</summary>
<p><em>Sourced from <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/releases">task-master-ai's releases</a>.</em></p>
<blockquote>
<h2>task-master-ai@0.41.0</h2>
<h3>Minor Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1571">#1571</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/c2d6c18a96fce5a2d5cb50bd1ae5d58ef577501c"><code>c2d6c18</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - Add loop command for automated task execution with Claude Code</p>
<p><strong>New Features:</strong></p>
<ul>
<li><code>task-master loop</code> command that runs Claude Code in a Docker sandbox, executing one task per iteration based on the selected tag</li>
<li>Built-in presets for different workflows:
<ul>
<li><code>default</code> - General task completion from the Task Master backlog</li>
<li><code>test-coverage</code> - Find uncovered code and write meaningful tests</li>
<li><code>linting</code> - Fix lint errors and type errors one by one</li>
<li><code>duplication</code> - Find duplicated code and refactor into shared utilities</li>
<li><code>entropy</code> - Find code smells and clean them up</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>Progress file tracking to maintain context across iterations (inside <code>.taskmaster/loop-progress.txt</code>)
<ul>
<li>Remember to delete this file between loops to not pollute the agent with bad context</li>
</ul>
</li>
<li>Automatic completion detection via <code>&lt;loop-complete&gt;</code> and <code>&lt;loop-blocked&gt;</code> markers</li>
</ul>
</li>
</ul>
<h3>Patch Changes</h3>
<ul>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1556">#1556</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/1befc6a341babd825b8dd000513ffbf8a1620e62"><code>1befc6a</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/TheLazyIndianTechie"><code>@​TheLazyIndianTechie</code></a>! - fix: tolerate AI SDK versions without jsonSchema export</p>
<p>Fallback to sanitized Zod schema handling when jsonSchema is unavailable, and
align structured-output tests and registration perf thresholds to reduce CI
failures.</p>
<p>Also enforce sequential, unique subtask ids when regenerating subtasks during
scope adjustment.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1553">#1553</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/226678b93aa01d0e62c0fac852802e9955c7ebd7"><code>226678b</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/Crunchyman-ralph"><code>@​Crunchyman-ralph</code></a>! - fix: Remove .default() from Zod schemas to satisfy OpenAI strict JSON schema validation</p>
<p>This fixes an issue where codex-cli provider (using OpenAI API) would fail with &quot;Missing 'dependencies'&quot; error during task expansion. OpenAI's structured outputs require all properties to be in the 'required' array, but Zod's .default() makes fields optional. The fix removes .default() from schemas and applies defaults at the application level instead.</p>
</li>
<li>
<p><a href="https://redirect.github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/pull/1543">#1543</a> <a href="https://github.com/eyaltoledano/claude-task-master/commit/9a6fa1bd2ab389097f1074fe4a4f779dee8180b6"><code>9a6fa1b</code></a> Thanks <a href="https://github.com/triepod-ai"><code>@​triepod-ai</code></a>! - feat: Add tool annotations for improved LLM tool understanding</p>
<p>Added MCP tool annotations (readOnlyHint, destructiveHint, title) to all 12 tools to help LLMs better understand tool behavior and make safer decisions about tool execution.</p>
</li>
</ul>
</blockquote>

<h2>Greptile Overview</h2>

### Greptile Summary

This PR updates the `task-master-ai` devDependency from version 0.40.1 to 0.41.0 in the minor-and-patch group. This is an automated Dependabot PR that bumps the version to include new features and bug fixes from the upstream package.

## What Changed

The update from 0.40.1 to 0.41.0 includes:

**New Features (Minor):**
- New `task-master loop` command for automated task execution with Claude Code in a Docker sandbox
- Built-in presets for different workflows (default, test-coverage, linting, duplication, entropy)
- Progress file tracking at `.taskmaster/loop-progress.txt`
- Automatic completion detection via markers

**Bug Fixes (Patch):**
- Tolerance for AI SDK versions without jsonSchema export
- Removal of `.default()` from Zod schemas for OpenAI strict JSON schema validation compatibility
- Added MCP tool annotations for improved LLM tool understanding
- Sequential, unique subtask IDs during scope adjustment

## Critical Issue Found

**Missing Lock File Update**: The `bun.lock` file was not updated in this PR. While `package.json` specifies `task-master-ai@^0.41.0`, the lock file still pins the dependency to version `0.40.1`. This mismatch means that running `bun install` will install version 0.40.1 instead of the intended 0.41.0, defeating the purpose of this dependency update.

## Compatibility Assessment

Based on the changelog, version 0.41.0 is a **minor version bump** that includes:

**New Features:**
- New `task-master loop` command for automated task execution
- Built-in presets for workflows (test-coverage, linting, duplication, entropy)
- Progress file tracking in `.taskmaster/loop-progress.txt`
- Automatic completion detection markers

**Bug Fixes:**
- Tolerance for AI SDK versions without jsonSchema export
- Removal of `.default()` from Zod schemas for OpenAI compatibility
- Tool annotations for improved LLM understanding

**Impact on this project:**
- This is a **devDependency** used only for development task management
- The project only uses the `tasks:list` command via `bunx task-master list`
- No breaking changes are documented in the release notes
- The new features (loop command, MCP tool annotations, Zod schema fixes) are additive and should not affect existing usage
- The patch fixes improve compatibility with different AI SDK versions and OpenAI API validation

### Confidence Score: 1/5

- This PR cannot be merged safely due to a critical lock file mismatch that will cause incorrect dependency installation
- The confidence score of 1/5 reflects a critical issue that blocks this PR from being merged: the bun.lock file was not updated to reflect the package.json change. This means the dependency version specified in package.json (0.41.0) will not actually be installed - instead, the old version (0.40.1) locked in bun.lock will be used. This defeats the entire purpose of the dependency update and could lead to confusion when developers expect new features from 0.41.0 but get the old version instead. While the dependency update itself appears safe (it's a devDependency with no breaking changes), the incomplete PR makes it unsafe to merge.
- package.json requires bun.lock to be regenerated and included in the PR
